# KOTOR 1: super-deep character write-ups (Oct 19, 2026)

## Jolee Bindo
*Corpus coverage: 530 own lines (every line, no length filter) · 32 lines naming them by name, alias or epithet · 5 pronoun references resolved from context · 148 lines addressed to them · 26 narration lines · 48 duplicate/variant lines merged (noted inline). Every «quote» is script-verified.*
### Background and history
Jolee is an old Jedi hermit living in Kashyyyk's Shadowlands. He fought in the Great Sith War era. His wife Nayama fell to the dark side: «My wife's name was Nayama.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]. He failed to kill her, and she went on to kill Jedi: «she went on to kill many Jedi during the war until she, herself, was slain in the final battle» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]. He remained a Padawan: «I had remained a Padawan throughout the war.» [K1/Hangar].
### Personality and motivations
Wry and skeptical of the Council's dogma: «Love doesn't lead to the dark side.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo].
### Arc (scene by scene)
- **Kashyyyk:** joins after the force field.
- **Manaan:** comments on the trial.
- **Dark ending:** fights Revan on the Temple Summit: «I'm a Jedi... I'll never bow down to the Sith.» [K1/Temple_Summit].
### Relationships
- Nayama.
- Revan.
- Bastila (critiques her).
- Davin Kotras (side quest).
### Abilities
- Jedi Consular-type; healing and lightsaber.
### Feats
- Survived the Great Sith War.
- Says of Nayama: «it was a long and terrible battle, but I defeated her» (per the first-pass notes; see the quote bank) [K1/Jolee_Bindo].
### Power-scaling statements
- Jolee on the Sith being cyclical (see the Hangar bank).
- Jolee on Malak and Bastila: «He'll want to use her battle meditation against the Republic.» [K1/Hangar].
### Duels and fights
- vs Revan, dark ending (loses).
### Verdict implications
- Veteran mid-tier.
- Confidence: medium.
### Open questions
- Nayama's strength is not quantified.

**[K1/Ahto_West]**
- «Be careful here, kid. Start poking your nose in places it doesn't belong and you might not like what you find.» [K1/Ahto_West]

- «Sounds like the Sith are trying to win this planet by corrupting the Selkath youth. Much more subtle than their usual methods.» [K1/Ahto_West]
- «Nice. Real nice. Maybe later we could find some insects to pull the legs off, huh?» [K1/Ahto_West] *(+2 exact-duplicate / he-she variant line(s) merged)*
- «Hey, kid, that was a good thing you did there. Almost brought a tear to this old man's eye.» [K1/Ahto_West]

**[K1/Anchorhead]**
- «My, my. You should be proud of yourself, outwitting her like that.» [K1/Anchorhead]
- «You've got a real mean streak, kid. You might want to work on that; nasty people tend to come to nasty ends.» [K1/Anchorhead]
- «*sigh* And how many more like you are there out there, with no one to buy their plates, I wonder?» [K1/Anchorhead]
- «I know I'm just an old codger, but in my time I've learned what goes around comes around. These things have a way of coming back to you in the end.» [K1/Anchorhead] *(+2 exact-duplicate / he-she variant line(s) merged)*
- «I'm thinking they're with the Sand People, who live in the south. Either that or I need my ears cleaned.» [K1/Anchorhead]
- «That seems pretty convenient, but it won't hurt to look. We *are* going to look, right?» [K1/Anchorhead]
- «Oh, I get it. He's small and helpless so we can just do whatever we want, right? Sometimes I just don't know about you, kid.» [K1/Anchorhead]

**[K1/Bastila]**
- «Yep, I figured it was only a matter of time until we had the whole "come back to the Order" discussion. Well, I guess there's no avoiding it now, so let's get it over with.» [K1/Bastila] *(+1 exact-duplicate / he-she variant line(s) merged)*
- «Well, I've managed to avoid it the last 20 years or so. Besides, light side, dark side: they don't mean the same to me as they do to you. I don't see in absolutes.» [K1/Bastila] *(+1 exact-duplicate / he-she variant line(s) merged)*
- «I want to stop Malak as much as anyone. But I don't have to join the Order to do it. Look at Carth, or Canderous. They're with us in this quest, but they aren't Jedi. The capacity for good or evil, like the Force itself, is in all living creatures. And belonging to the Jedi Order, or the Sith, or any group, won't change what you are at your core.» [K1/Bastila] *(+1 exact-duplicate / he-she variant line(s) merged)*
- «Yeah, I'm old and stubborn. But I appreciate the effort. But from now on you can just think of me as any other non-Jedi in our little group - with a lightsaber. And Force powers.» [K1/Bastila] *(+1 exact-duplicate / he-she variant line(s) merged)*

**[K1/Ebon_Hawk]**

- «Hmph. I suppose I could whip up something, uh... you know... few herbs and such. You do know that the best alternative is not to get hurt, right? Not to say that you go leaping into the path of blaster fire or anything, but I know how you young people get. Here, take it.» [K1/Ebon_Hawk]
- «How many could you possibly need? And besides, I don't have enough supplies on me to put some more together. Ask me later when you're actually in need and I'm less tired. I'm not a kit factory, you know.» [K1/Ebon_Hawk]
- «Stop bothering me with your questions! I can barely hear myself think.» [K1/Ebon_Hawk]

**[K1/Hall_of_the_Chieftain]**
- «Wait... isn't that my line?» [K1/Hall_of_the_Chieftain]

- «Madam, I am not a fool. I survive because I am careful.» [K1/Hall_of_the_Chieftain]

**[K1/Hangar]**
- «Where is Bastila? What happened on that ship?» [K1/Hangar]
- «Bah. Malak won't kill her, don't be foolish. He'll want to use her battle meditation against the Republic. Turn her to the dark side and the Sith will always be victorious.» [K1/Hangar]
- «What about me? I already knew who you were, though it wasn't my place to tell you. Better off that you know, if you ask me. Does it change anything? I'm not here to judge you. You'll do what you have to, and I'll help if I can.» [K1/Hangar]
- «Think you'd never heard of the Force before. Seems obvious enough to me it was meant to be.» [K1/Hangar]
- «No, no, of course not. The Sith have come and gone for ages. They were not called Sith many thousands of years ago, perhaps, but the dark side was always present without a doubt. Oh, sure, occasionally the light side comes close to vanquishing the dark, but the dark always returns. The fact that Exar Kun was defeated didn't mean the Sith would never return, as they obviously have now. Everyone knows that.» [K1/Hangar] *(+1 exact-duplicate / he-she variant line(s) merged)*
- «Ah. Exar was a Jedi who was... corrupted... by ghosts of the old Sith. Or so they say. He attempted to conquer the Republic and create a new Golden Age of the Sith.» [K1/Hangar] *(+1 exact-duplicate / he-she variant line(s) merged)*
- «Better to say he was defeated... but essentially, yes. The victory did not come easily, however.» [K1/Hangar] *(+1 exact-duplicate / he-she variant line(s) merged)*
- «I was an experienced Padawan, surely... but not yet ready to be a full Jedi and certainly not ready to train another. Especially not one so old as my wife.» [K1/Hangar]
- «I did. I wasn't the first and I won't be the last. The problem with self-righteous folk is they think they're more right than everyone else.» [K1/Hangar] *(+1 exact-duplicate / he-she variant line(s) merged)*
- «It was a scene being repeated everywhere throughout the galaxy. Pupil against master. In my case... it was a long and terrible battle, but I defeated her.» [K1/Hangar]
- «No... no. I had her at my mercy, disarmed and defenseless. She looked up at me and she knew... she knew I couldn't do it.» [K1/Hangar] *(+1 exact-duplicate / he-she variant line(s) merged)*
- «But I should have. Sometimes I convince myself otherwise... but it's no use. She had fallen to the dark side when she raised her saber against me.» [K1/Hangar] *(+1 exact-duplicate / he-she variant line(s) merged)*
- «I had trained Nayama against their wishes. I had failed to kill her when I had the chance, and she went on to kill others. Not to mention that I had remained a Padawan throughout the war. A formality, perhaps, but with the trial it had to be decided if I was worthy to become a Jedi at all. It was a travesty, of course. I told you that even the Jedi were capable of great injustices, didn't I?» [K1/Hangar]
- «But I deserved to be tried. They found me innocent.» [K1/Hangar]
- «Well, welcome to my home, such as it is. Pull up a stump and be comfortable. We should discuss a few things.» [K1/Hangar] *(+1 exact-duplicate / he-she variant line(s) merged)*
- «Ah, what is there to tell? Jolee Bindo is the crazy old man in the dangerous woods. I'm content with the impression I give.» [K1/Hangar] *(+1 exact-duplicate / he-she variant line(s) merged)*
- «I follow the Jedi Way and I command the Force, yes, and I suppose that makes me as much a Jedi as anyone... but what of it?» [K1/Hangar]
- «Does it change anything? I'm not here to judge you. You'll do what you have to, and I'll help if I can.» [K1/Hangar]
- «I've seen my share of the dark and the light. And frankly, both extremes annoy me. Of course... I have felt the rumblings of change...» [K1/Hangar] *(+1 exact-duplicate / he-she variant line(s) merged)*
- «Yes, there's the thing. Obstinate machine. I've no doubt it holds what you seek, but good luck getting it operational.» [K1/Hangar] *(+1 exact-duplicate / he-she variant line(s) merged)*
- «Match found...? What the... it always muttered something about "rejected patterns" for me.» [K1/Hangar] *(+1 exact-duplicate / he-she variant line(s) merged)*
- «Heh... call me stubborn, I guess. Heh. There wasn't much else to do around here.» [K1/Hangar] *(+1 exact-duplicate / he-she variant line(s) merged)*

**[K1/Hunting_Lodge]**
- «Ugh. Must we stand so close to them? I'd forgotten what Gamorreans smelled like.» [K1/Hunting_Lodge]
- «[Force Persuade] Just answer the lady's questions.» [K1/Hunting_Lodge]
- «I thought it obvious. Should he act like that after mentioning his wife? Greasy little wrench-rat. He asked for it.» [K1/Hunting_Lodge]
- «I have no doubt. I just don't like him. Mentions his wife and then acts like that.» [K1/Hunting_Lodge]
- «I only meant to help him clear his thoughts. He's confused if he'll mention a wife and then act like that... the greasy little scavenger.» [K1/Hunting_Lodge]
- «Hmph. Hunting a species to extinction. Now there's a logical way to make a living.» [K1/Hunting_Lodge]

**[K1/Jolee_Bindo]**
- «Well, I'll be a Wookiee's uncle. Davin! Davin Kotras!» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I crashed somewhere and decided to stay. Heh heh.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «You're too kind. Not the young lad anymore yourself, I see.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Yes, yes, right you are. Sorry, Davin, but duty calls. Maybe I'll run into you again soon.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «What, are you deaf? His name's Davin. Old friend from my wanderer days. By the by, Davin, I really should be running off...» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «No. Why? What's there to hear?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «That's preposterous.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Perhaps I will at that. It's up to my friend, here, actually. I'm just tagging along.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I'd appreciate that. Sunry's an old friend... I'd hate to see him hurt.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I was expecting that. Still... if we get the chance, I'd be grateful. Sunry's an old friend... I'd hate to see him hurt.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I will. Be safe, old friend.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «What? You think because I'm a hermit I've always been a hermit? Just because I haven't seen Sunry for twenty years doesn't mean he's not a friend. A man could have changed a lot in that span of time, though. But to become a murderer? Oh. Mm mm mm. I can't see it. That's not the Sunry I knew. He was a good man. Put me up in his home for a year back after I left the order. War hero, too, or so I hear. I wouldn't mind helping the fellow, if we can. I owe it to him. Or you can be a young monkey-lizard and ignore me, I guess... well, I don't expect much these days.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «[Even though he has joined your group, Jolee remains a somewhat mysterious character. Maybe you should speak to him about why he chose to come with you.]» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «[You decide to ignore Jolee and continue on with your quest for now, though it might be a good idea to speak with him later.]» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «You got yourself a fast little ship. Heh. I'd forgotten what engines sounded like. The closest thing to that on Kashyyyk is an uller in mating season. Ugh! Frightful.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Or it could be for the free food. What's the gunk that comes out of the synthesizer on this bucket, anyway? Do you never clean the darned thing?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I'm old, damn it! I'm allowed to be enigmatic when I want to be, and don't go telling me otherwise.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «How impatient can one person be? You must have driven your mother mad. All that gurgling and fussing... heh, babies are cute, but annoying.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Wow, you're not much for interrogation, are you? Is this as hard as you try? Remind me to sign up for your next torture session.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «You know, you remind me of someone else I knew ages ago. Pleasant enough fellow, great destiny... all of that. Breath like a bantha.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Oh ho ho, very funny. Is it my fault that some people are so easily annoyed? They're like impatient little children. With blasters.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «No, but it could be. An Anduvian salt tablet would clear that right up, you know.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «So I am. Tell you what... when you get to be my age, you can go ahead and answer any question you like, too. You have my permission.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Anyway, where was I? Oh, yes. Andor Vex, was his name. The Force swirled around him like a hurricane, that's how great his destiny was.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «No, you wouldn't have. Sometimes swirling Force is just swirling Force. It gets us old Jedis excited at our age so we go "ooo, destiny!"» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Well, it's not so much a hurricane as a slight breeze. But that doesn't mean anything: destiny is sometimes in the eye of the beholder.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I did. Just because someone has the Force swirling about them doesn't always mean they have a great destiny, but it doesn't hurt to check it out.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Well, it turned out that poor Andor believed a wee bit too much in the infallibility of that destiny. That overconfidence turned out to be his downfall.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I don't know. Are you overconfident? I hadn't noticed. Even if I had, I would never comment on it. We're talking about Andor, remember?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Does it have to have a point? I thought we were just talking about Andor. Is something wrong with your attention span?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I am not! I've no need to make things up, with my poor memory and all. You just sit still and let me finish, damn it.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Let's see... oh, yes. Andor's downfall. I was pretty young, myself, when it happened. At the time, I thought that Andor's destiny couldn't be more boring.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Well, let's just say that I was a strapping young lad with a full head of hair and Coruscant was a small town with a well, heh heh.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «You know, if you keep interrupting, it will take even longer. Have you no patience at all? How did you get as old as you are anyway?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Well, he had a much better food dispenser than you do. That and the fact that even then I wasn't an altogether impatient *twit*.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I was just about to abandon Andor to whatever the Force intended for him when his ship was overtaken by a Dimean warship. Now, you've probably never heard of the Dimeans, but at the time they were a nasty lot led by a nastier overlord named Kraat. Tall fellow. Big teeth. Kraat has us hauled onto the bridge of his ship for questioning, and that's when I knew that Andor's destiny was at hand.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Of course he did! Haven't you been listening? It was not in the way you'd probably expect, though.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Swirling Force, remember? Jedi here? Granted, I was just interpreting the signs, but we get trained in that sort of thing. More or less.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Well, Andor decides that his destiny makes him invulnerable and starts making all sorts of demands. Free me now, I'm not answering questions, blah blah blah. Don't you know who I am? Kraat decides he's had enough and begins crushing Andor's neck. I told the boy he should have kept his mouth shut. I think he agreed, too... or those could have just been gurgling noises. Well... well, anyway. Finally, Kraat has enough of Andor and tosses him aside into this giant energy intake shaft. Andor gets sucked in and starts bouncing around, heh, screaming... heh. Maybe Andor hit something sensitive on the way down or just didn't agree with the reactor core, next thing I know all the ship's alarms are ringing.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Everyone panics and I run, barely making it to the ship in time before the explosion. Kraat dies horribly, and the Dimeans never quite recovered. Changed the political course of the entire sector for centuries to come. I'd call that quite a destiny, wouldn't you?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Well, now, that's the ingratitude of youth for you. I relate an exciting tale and do I get thanked for it? No, no, it's all 'me me me!' Hmph.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «What?! Are you kidding?! What are the odds of that happening, anyway? A billion to one? You should do so well as to be sucked into the engine of some evil Sith Lord, you know. Andor was a hero... sort of.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Well, hey, the chances may not be great, but when one has the opportunity to see something like that twice in a lifetime...» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Anyway, go on. My throat is dry and you're making me cranky. Shoo!» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «No, I never did, did I?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Of course you do. I know things, remember? You think you could make it around this part of the galaxy without me? Don't answer that.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Elusive? Me, elusive? Heh. Obviously you've never tried to grab a Twi'lek dancing girl after drinking too much Ondaran willek juice.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «My, my, somebody woke up on the wrong side of the starship this morning, I see. Is that a chip on your shoulder or are you a bit surly today?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «At any rate, I already told you why I wanted to leave. I've seen everything I wanted to on Kashyyyk. Time to go, time to move on.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «To bed. I'm old and tired, you know.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «The hermit says he's tired of living alone in the giant forest and you find that hard to believe? How cynical are you?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «You live in an overgrown stump with the tachs beating down your door and you tell me how happy you would be. I was pleased just to have visitors.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «How many kilometer-high trees can you find an interest in before you figure you've had enough? I'd bet you can't be bothered to stay in one room for more than fifteen minutes. And then there's all the critters in the Shadowlands... I'm just happy to be back in space, doing something new. Is that too much to ask?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «So I could. Not having a ship does tend to put a damper on that kind of idea, however.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «You got a smart mouth on you there, you know that? If ullers could talk, they'd sound like that.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «There, now, was that so hard? An old man has to be allowed some petty eccentricities. Nice to see you agree, for once.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I'll admit... for all its flaws, Kashyyyk was home enough. But when you came along and I saw the destiny you had before you, I couldn't help but be intrigued.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Of course not. Weren't you listening last time? I can see that you have a destiny before you... but the details are far from clear.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Interest? Well, I certainly don't have a vested interest... call it more idle curiosity. Your destiny is... rather unclear.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Have I offered a single opinion? No, I haven't, though you could do far worse than hear a couple of mine. Your destiny, after all, is rather unclear.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «In fact, everything about you that I can see is odd. Slightly off, as if my eyes are trying to trick me. Something... something is very dark about you... ... but bah! I'm sure you don't need to hear my ruminations. You've probably got enough nosy Jedi offering you one opinion after another to make you sick.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Your future will come of its own accord, trust me. Sometimes navel-gazing at it doesn't get you anywhere.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Well I for one am quite content to let you make your own mistakes. Just because I want to see where this ends up doesn't mean I need to tell you how to get there.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I'll remind you that *you're* the one who started pumping me for information. Like an old man doesn't have anything better to do. Hmph.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I wouldn't worry about it too much, if I were you. You remind me a bit of Nomi, heh heh, and that can't be all bad.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Nomi Sunrider. She came late to the Force, just as you have, and became one of the greatest Jedi ever. Oh, fine, fine lass. Whether you'll follow the same path remains to be seen. For one thing, we'll never get anywhere if you stand around chatting up old men all day...» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Oh, stop being such a baby. Are you always so quick to turn aside an ally? Got too many of them, do you?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Nonsense. Have I ever refused to help you when you asked? How confused can one person be, anyway?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Good, since I didn't offer to be one. You think anyone would want to mentor you with that kind of attitude? Not me, I'm past that.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «One thing I will say is that this little escapade does remind me a bit of my adventuring days before the war. Ahh, those were exciting times. Or at least it would remind me of those times if we didn't stand around. What's keeping you? You're too young to be so talkative. Shoo! Shoo!» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Did I say that? Strange the tricks memory plays on you when you get older.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Didn't I say that my past was my affair? You don't see me poking and prodding you with questions, do you?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I'm not here to satisfy your curiosity! No staring at the old man, that's what the sign says, dammit!» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Hmph. I might be. But a mouthy young thing like yourself shouldn't get to call me an old coot, dammit.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «And besides... you don't really want to hear about me. We're talking ancient history, probably before you were born. History bores kids. Proven fact.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Well that's a relief. Maybe an old man can finally get some peace around here. Now, run along and do whatever you were doing, already.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Oh fine, fine, have it your way. Just don't cry about it later.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «So you're not. Fine, have it your way. Just don't say I didn't warn you.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Yes, yes, I was an adventurer. Happy now? I wasn't even done with my Jedi training back then. I had a full head of hair and an eagerness to see absolutely everything. Sound familiar? The Council was never very happy with willful, brash Jolee Bindo, you see. Even less so when I began my smuggling career.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Don't look at me like that, dammit! I wasn't always the wrinkled coot I am now, you know. I can still fight, too, so wipe off that smirk I see there.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «You're very sure of that, are you? My, my, you must be very learned and experienced to know so many things. At any rate, I was still a Padawan at the time.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «At the time the Ukatis system was interdicted by its own King. He preferred to keep his people starving and poor, all the better to oppress them. The Senate was trying to negotiate a peace, but they were getting nowhere as usual. I decided I wasn't going to wait. I found myself a ship and a partner and we began smuggling food and supplies to the Ukatis citizenry through the blockade.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Oh, it was. I was a half-decent pilot in those days... and with the Force guiding me, we made it through some tough spots nobody else would have.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Pilot, smuggler... several other things, too. Or did you suppose I was always a crotchety hermit?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Why not? There were millions of people crying out for aid. What else was I supposed to do?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Don't be stupid. Are you doing nothing? If nothing's not good enough for you there's no reason it should have been good enough for me back then, either.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Well... we didn't *buy* all the equipment, per se. Some were happy to donate goods. Some we just, ah, knew had more than they could use...» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «'Stole' is such a harsh word. They would have donated those goods readily enough if they were compassionate. I considered it a tax on the greedy. We only got caught once. A lone Ukatish frigate shot us down and forced a crash landing. I thought the Force had abandoned me, as I remember.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Oh, so you're going to speak for the Force now, are you? I deserved to crash, is that it?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Hmph. As I recall, I'm not the only person in this conversation who's been shot down out of the sky recently.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Well, as it happens, getting shot down turned out to be very fortunate. That day was the day I...» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Well, that... that was the day I met my wife.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «It's alright. When you're digging through the trash, you shouldn't be surprised when you encounter something unpleasant.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «You know another way to get a wife? But, yes... that's when I met her.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «No. No, that was a good memory. There are just... much worse ones that followed.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I... If it's all the same to you, I'd prefer to stop talking now. My mouth is starting to draw flies.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I don't want to talk about that.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Maybe, but I doubt it. I'm made stubborn that way.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Now that's a unique method of getting someone to talk. Thunder about like a bantha in a circuit shop. Does that work for you often?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Yes you do. You may mean well enough, but my private affairs are just that: private.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Let me tell you something. Once you've lived as many years as I have, you'll have yourself a long, long list of memories. If you're lucky, most of them will be good. If you're not, some will be bad. If you're really unlucky, some will be so bad you never want to be reminded of them again... ever. You'll go far away, to a place that doesn't hold any memories at all. And there you'll be happy just to forget and be forgotten.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «You say that now, but I'm talking to you from across the breadth of many years experience. That is a gap I simply can't cross for you.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Heh. Partly, maybe. I doubt I could ever explain it to you fully, even if I wanted to.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «There are worse things to call yourself, for better reasons. If you don't think it's even possible that you might be flinging those words at yourself one day, you're younger than I thought.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Let me ask you this: have you ever been in love? Truly in love, I mean, and not simple infatuation.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Exactly. You're still at the beginning of your life. There will be women in your life... perhaps many women... but if you're fortunate you'll find love once.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Exactly. You're still at the beginning of your life. There will be men in your life... perhaps many men... but if you're fortunate you'll find love once.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «The Jedi, with their damnable sense of over-caution, would tell you love is something to avoid. Thankfully, anyone who's even partially alive knows that's not true.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «And why not? All this nonsense about avoiding love is so much foo-foo. I shouldn't be the only one who realizes that.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Love doesn't lead to the dark side. Passion can lead to rage and fear, and can be controlled... but passion is not the same thing as love. Controlling your passions while being in love... that's what they should teach you to beware. But love, itself, will save you... not condemn you. Ahhhh, but listen to me go on as if I had all the answers. What do I know of love anymore? I'm just a lonely old man who's not even a Jedi.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Don't I? Hmph. Maybe you're right. It seems whatever I have to say is falling on deaf ears, anyhow. So go on, go on, run amok and kiss the bantha for all I care, I won't comment on it.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Hmph. Maybe. Do you want to avoid the greatest things in life simply because they come with some complications?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «You do, do you? I wouldn't listen too closely... I'm no authority on anything. I just think that the greatest things in life shouldn't be avoided because they come with a few complications.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Nope. Never was. Technically I never rose above the rank of Padawan, for various reasons. Does that surprise you?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Heh. I suppose I'm not prone to live my life like most of the Jedi Masters do, that's true. My opinions on love are no different.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «In my case, becoming a Jedi would have been a formality. I doubt the order and I would have gotten along well, considering my opinions on things such as love.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «No I didn't. I may never have become a Jedi technically, but I'm an old Padawan with a lifetime of experience. That applies both to the Force as well as topics like love.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Love causes pain, certainly. Inevitably love is going to lead to as much sorrow and regret as it does joy. I suppose there are perfect, eternal loves out there... but I haven't seen any. How you deal with the bad part of love is what determines your character, what determines the dark side's hold over you.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I suppose it could. It would take a strong person to make that kind of commitment, I think. Someone with a great sense of self.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Bah. A life without risk is boring. Is that how you want to live? You want love, you've got to fight for it.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I haven't changed my mind. I'm still not going to talk about it. You go and find your own love if you want to know so badly.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I'll tell you one thing: sometimes, no matter how hard you try, you and the one you love simply aren't meant to be together. The trick is to know when that is, to know when it's time to fight and when it's time to part ways. Hmph... there I go waxing philosophical again. Somebody blast me already! Let's get going before I start talking in riddles, dammit!» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Heh heh. Who said I left the Jedi?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Well, technically, I was only a Padawan... not that that makes a difference to most. But as for the order, itself... no, I never left it. It left *me*.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Don't get so uppity, dammit! The point I was trying to make, if you decide to actually listen, is that I never left the order. The order left *me*.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Oh, is it? Hmph. There are moments when I think you must be much older than you seem... but this isn't one of them.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Something wrong with your ears? I thought folks only went deaf when they got to my age. I follow the Jedi Way and use the Force, don't I? That makes me a Jedi last time I checked. But the order, itself, the Jedi Council and so forth... no, I'm not a part of that and haven't been for a long time. And good riddance, I say!» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Hmph. A rogue, am I? You speak as if there must be something wrong with me, like not being part of the order makes me a criminal.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «You know what I hate? Well... you know, lots of things, really. But I'm old and easily annoyed. But that's beside the point. What I *really* hate are how most people view the Jedi. Everyone thinks the Jedi are perfect, that they can do no wrong. They think the Jedi Council is completely incapable of injustice.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Hmph. I guess you aren't as stupid as you sometimes act. No doubt you've been on the receiving end of Jedi justice at least once, eh?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Damn right I disagree. The Jedi are just as capable of injustice as anyone else. They may *try* harder, sure, but sometimes they get it wrong.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Quiet, you. I'm not talking about me. Talking about the Jedi as a whole. They are just as capable of imperfection and injustice as anyone else, as you can obviously see.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «And I'm not even talking about how some of us fall to the dark side. No, that's plenty indication of our fallibility, but it's something else entirely. No, I'm talking about how, more often than not, your average robe-wearing Jedi can try to do the right thing and still be completely *wrong*.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «*sigh* I suppose I'm not being very clear, am I?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «No, no. The Jedi always treated me well. It would be foolish and untrue to say otherwise. That's not what I meant, anyway.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «That's true, but it's not what I meant. I guess I'm not being clear, am I?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Come to think of it, I don't have to be clear. Someone my age is entitled to ramble, dammit! But for your sake I'll try to explain. I'll tell you a little tale about a Jedi Master I once knew. Hortath, I think. Or was it Hartoth? I could never get it straight...» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «You're the one who was asking me about the Jedi, dammit! Now do you want the story or not?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Boring you, am I? Well you just keep your questions to yourself next time. No need for old men to go and make nuisances of themselves.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «*Sigh* You know the problem with the youth of today? They're young.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Where was I, then? Oh. Oh, yes... Master Hortath. He was a kindly old Jedi who meant well, but the most near-sighted thing in the Core, I swear. He would walk into walls, knock over tables, mistake apprentices for rancor beasts... that sort of thing. And he was too proud to submit to proper treatment. Some used to counsel him and urged "Use the Force, Master Hortath. Allow the Force to see for you." But he refused to believe that his eyes were failing. He simply squinted more and more as the years went on, the other Jedi resignedly passing it off as the amusing quirk of a compassionate old man.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I'm not finished yet! Now shush.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «So one day a young Padawan meets Master Hortath in the courtyard and, not knowing of his blindness, asks him for directions to the Council. Quite sure of himself, Hortath gave the lad directions... which happened to lead outside and away from the enclave. The Padawan is confused, naturally. He asks if Master Hortath is sure, and of course Master Hortath says that he is. The Padawan suggests that perhaps he should ask someone else... but the proud Hortath now feels insulted. He tells the Padawan to take the route he prescribed and no other. Rather dejectedly, the Padawan did as he was told... and so ended up leaving the Jedi Order forever. It was decided that the boy's fate was to leave the Order anyway... though whether that was out of respect for Hortath or because the boy went on to something else, well, we'll never know.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Not much. I never knew the Padawan nor met Master Hortath, himself. He was before my time.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «No, no. Both of them were from before my time. Well before the Sith Wars, even.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Considering that it's about blindness, I think that's an appropriate response.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «The tale is about blindness and I thought the point was clear. At any rate, you think about it.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «You're the one who asked why the Jedi left me, remember? Now let's get going. My feet are itching for a good run.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Bad, bad men. Women, too, to be fair.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Oh, indeed. They make a fine sandwich, also. Heh heh heh. But don't tell the Jedi Council I said that.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Who's playing games? Ask the next Sith you see if they're bad and they'll set you straight.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «*sigh* And just what gave you the impression that I know anything more about the Sith than you do?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Oh... that's right. Damn the ears of the young! I was expecting you to be your usual inattentive self when I mentioned that. So it's true, yes, I fought plenty of Sith. That was during the time of Exar Kun... oh, forty years ago now? Has it been that long?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «A Sith believes he commands the Force... but it is the dark side who commands him. You know this, there is little I could truly add.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «That... is not a pleasant time to remember. After Exar Kun fell to the dark side, he attempted to recruit other Jedi to his cause. What surprised us... what took us completely unprepared... was how utterly successful he was. Many Jedi joined him and became Sith, themselves. Why they did I... I will never truly know. But they did. Battle broke out throughout the order... pupil against master, we fought ourselves...» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I've often held the opinion that history has one basic flaw: it is seldom heeded. So go on and greet the world with your spite. Let's see where it takes you. You certainly don't need to hear any more from me.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Yes. More than difficult... next to impossible. How do you fight against someone you love?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Easy now to call them traitors. Tell me... how do you fight against someone that you love?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Bah. I dislike such memories. It leaves a taste in the mouth that... it is a sadness I thought I had put aside long ago. Ask me again about the war some other time. Just... not now. I would prefer to be by myself for now.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Not particularly.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Hmm. Such a charming and persuasive manner you have there. Forget it!» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «[Failure] *Sigh* I'm not interested in your demands. I'll tell my story when I'm damn good and ready... which isn't now, so shoo!» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «[Success] I suppose you're going to nag me until I cough it up, aren't you? Nothing is private anymore, it looks like. *sigh* There's no escaping it, I guess. So be it. My wife's name was Nayama. She was the Ukatis enforcer who shot me out of the sky, if you remember.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Bah. You got ears that work, don't you? I was a smuggler, way way back. I got shot down once over Ukatis. THAT story, yes.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «No I'm not senile, dammit! Not yet, anyway. Hmmm... well at least I don't think so. At any rate, I'll tell the story the way I want to.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «My wife had plenty to do with the war. Upon meeting her, I knew right away that she was strong in the Force. That's why she was able to shoot me down. Nayama was a marvel of a woman. Fiery, determined, smart... she dragged me to the capital and foiled three of my attempts to escape prison. Oh, and that body...» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Well... yes, that. Needless to say I eventually won her over. That was after I kidnapped her upon being broken out of the Ukatis prison, mind you. But uh, that's another story entirely. At any rate, I wanted to train her in the Jedi way. The Council refused my request, naturally. I was still a Padawan at the time. I was an experienced Padawan, surely... but not yet ready to be a full Jedi and certainly not ready to train another. Especially not one so old as my wife.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Easy to say so now. At the time I just thought I was right.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Nayama was intrigued by the idea of becoming a Jedi. She liked the idea of power too much, perhaps, but I certainly didn't see that at the time.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I believed in her and trained her in secret. I ignored her willful nature... I loved her too much to see fault in her. And she loved me, too. I know she did. At the time, our love was a shared bliss... better than anything I had known before or since.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Exar Kun is what happened. Nayama was inspired by Exar's promises of a new Golden Age. She wanted to join him. She came to me, pleading with me to throw aside what she called the decrepit trappings of the Jedi... to join her in Exar's war.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I... hadn't thought so. Not right then. I was too proud to believe that of her. I had trained her myself... I loved her.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «No of course I didn't kill her. How could I? I pleaded with her to reconsider, to think about what she was doing.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I pleaded with her to reconsider, to think about all that she was throwing away... to think about what she would become.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «She would have none of it. Finally, in frustration, she attacked me. She drew her lightsaber and attempted to strike me down. It was a scene being repeated everywhere throughout the galaxy. Pupil against master. In my case... it was a long and terrible battle, but I defeated her.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Yes. I think she truly fell the moment she raised up her saber against me. I have played that instant in my head... so many times.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Was I? Sometimes I convince myself that I couldn't have done otherwise... that I couldn't have seen that she had truly fallen to the dark side. But, yes, I was a fool.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «And I let her go. To my shame, she went on to kill many Jedi during the war until she, herself, was slain in the final battle. I grieved for her death, inevitable as it was, even as the Jedi Council put me on trial for my actions once the war was over.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I had trained Nayama against their wishes. I had failed to kill her when I had the chance, and she went on to kill others. Not to mention that I had remained a Padawan throughout the war. A formality, perhaps, but with the trial it had to be decided if I was worthy to become a Jeid at all. It was a travesty, of course. I told you that even the Jedi were capable of great injustices, didn't I?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Not at all.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Of course I deserved to be punished.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «But I deserved to be tried.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «They found me innocent. Even though I... deserved every punishment and more... they let me go. Mitigating circumstances, they said. I deserved compassion, they said. They said I had learned wisdom the hard way. For all I had done during the war, they wished to raise me to full Jedi status at long last. That... that was when the Jedi left me. That was when they failed me.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «No. Maybe you don't, at that.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «For my pride? For my cowardice? Which do you think is the act more befitting a Jedi?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «They may have been able to forgive me. I... could never forgive myself.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I... Yes, I do, I suppose. Does that surprise you? Ahhh... it is so long ago. Lost in the winds, I suppose. Nobody cares what an old man believes anymore, do they? Let's continue on with the task at hand. I would prefer to think of the present today.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Oh, that was not until many years later, to tell the truth. I spent quite some time wandering the galaxy.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Why wouldn't they? I had refused my promotion to Jedi... I was a Padawan who had left the Order, nothing more. I traveled from one civilized system to the next, never staying long. I don't even think I knew what I was searching for.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Nothing so extravagant. I merely traveled from place to place... I don't even think I knew what I was searching for.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «It wasn't as if my travels were pleasant, either. There were plenty of folks who distrusted the Jedi after the war... or worse.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I suppose so. Not everyone would understand why the Jedi would suddenly start killing themselves and tearing up half the galaxy, would they?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I was lost. I couldn't conceive of returning to the Jedi, so what would you propose I'd done?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «If people weren't treating me with suspicion, they were looking at me with greed. I don't know how many thought they could make use of me for their own ends. I got so sick of the treachery and deceit I left the civilized parts of the galaxy and headed instead for the uncivilized parts.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Actually I was on my way somewhere else when I crash-landed on Kashyyyk. The ship I was using was a rust-bucket.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I'd taken some damage passing through an unexpected asteroid field, as I recall, but I wasn't completely without some systems. I could still guide her a bit when I crashed. It wasn't what I would call the smoothest landing, especially considering I ended up smack in the depths of the Shadowlands. But I lived.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Hmph. I'm no mechanic. And besides, after you plunge nose-first into the trunk of a five-kilometer high tree, chances are you don't have much ship left.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Sure, why not? It seemed like an interesting enough place to spend a couple of decades exploring.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Hmmm. That was a challenge at first. You've seen the kind of creatures that exist down there... and you missed the really big ones. I was still able to rely on the Force to keep me safe for the most part. The rest of the trick is keeping out of the way of most of the predators.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «No, that's true. Still... most of the creatures grew accustomed to me, and I to them. At least none of them ever heard of a Jedi.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Bah, what do you know? I'd done enough wandering by that point, thank you very much. I felt at peace in the Shadowlands.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «They did at first, oh yes. I can't say I was overly pleased to encounter a group of indigenous giant carpets either, I can assure you of that.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «No, no. I was the intruder in their land, after all. They got to trust me well enough after a couple of decades of my aid.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Well that was after two decades of helping them. They certainly didn't trust me at first.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «And why would I have been interested in that? You can't spend five minutes in a Wookiee village without coming out smelling like wet fur. No, no, let them rule themselves. They can screw up their history just like every other race before them. No, I helped them in other ways.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «When I could. I would assist the few young ones who would get lost in the Shadowlands or attacked unexpectedly by the wildlife. I must say... for a while there, the Wookiees actually thought I was some kind of benevolent forest god. Amusing, really. I set them straight eventually.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «At first, when the slavers took to hunting down lone Wookiees in the fringes of the Shadowlands, I did my best to divert them. Later, when Chuundar made his deal I didn't see any point. I wasn't here to save them from their own sad follies, after all.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Nonsense. I had no idea that's what they were thinking for a long time. I just thought they were being friendly neighbors, leaving fruit and such for me. Later, when I started to understand some of their grunting, I realized they would say prayers to the 'hairless one' before descending into the forest. Hmph. Hairless one. I used to have plenty of hair, I tell you. *Ahem* Well, at any rate, it took a few bruises, but I set the record straight.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I traveled to Rwookrrorro and met with the chieftain in front of everyone. Freyyr swatted at me just to see if I was real or not. I was real, and while it was a light swat, Wookiees are terribly strong. I was knocked out cold for a day. The Wookiees all thought it was quite funny.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Not really. Kashyyyk is a place you can feel very small in. It felt good to devote my time to helping people and living simply.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «What can I say? I did it all for the Wookiees.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «The Wookiees. Well, okay, maybe I needed some time on a quiet and remote planet... but if you ever need a friend, an incredibly strong hairball isn't a bad call.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Why, thank you. You're not so bad, yourself, all things considered.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I suppose I am, in a way, despite the smell. For a race of gardeners, they've developed quite interestingly.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «See? Now you know how I feel half the time.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «You remember the alien computer, correct? Kashyyyk was meant to be an agricultural planet. The Wookiees were made for a reason. Or, at least, that's what I'm thinking. But I'm an old man who's had a long time to develop that opinion, so don't argue with me.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «At any rate, we should be moving along, don't you think? If you sat around this long in the Shadowlands, a tach would eat you.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Are we back to *that* again?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Hmmm... what's the best way for me to approach this? Ah, perhaps it's time for a little story...» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «You just keep quiet there, you! I've had to put up with all your busy-body questions, haven't I? Well, now you'll listen to a story, dammit! *ahem* Now where was I? Oh yes, the story... you almost made me forget about it. Nice try, but I'm not that old just yet. Heh heh. Now, then. A young man sees a terribly venomous snake in his small village. Nervous, he watches the snake carefully until it leaves. The young man follows the snake into the forest. He clears the branches out of its path and helps it over obstacles. He even works to keep it fed.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Shush! Many nights pass and still the young man continues to follow the snake. He even follows it into the sands of the great desert. In the desert, the snake eventually grows hungry. It turns and bites the young man, its poison quickly working its way into his system. Finally curious, the snake looks at the boy as he lays dying and asks, "Why were you foolish enough to follow me all the way out into the desert?" The boy looks back and replies, "Did I follow you? I thought I was leading you away from everyone else..." And then he died.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Well, now, that's what I wanted to see for myself.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I've come a long way as it is, and I'm going to die anyway so that doesn't bother me in the least.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Well, then... let's hope you're not the young man, either.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «The real question is whether or not I'm as frightened as I should be, isn't it?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I've told you before that you have a destiny before you. This does not mean, however, that your future is already written. They are not the same thing. You have the choice of which direction you take your destiny in. More than engine-sucking Andor, certainly... but even he had a choice.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «So far you've chosen to take a darker path. You know the consequences of that... we'll see if you decide to stay your course.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «So far you've chosen to take the lighter path. Can you stay that course, even through the challenges ahead? We'll have to wait and see.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «I'm not here to judge you or tell you which path to take. I'm here ready to offer you my help... should you ask for it. I do that because I think it's important. More important than remaining in my home and pretending the galaxy doesn't exist. That's why I'm here.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Heh. I'm rather glad I came, too, really. You're a fine young lad. I hope... I hope things turn out well for you.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Heh. I'm rather glad I came, too, really. You're a fine young lass. I hope... I hope things turn out well for you.» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Hmph. So you say, but it's my time to waste. Perhaps I can influence your decision, perhaps not... only time will tell, won't it?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «That's very kind of you, but while elsewhere may be safer, elsewhere isn't where I think I should be. Besides... what's an old man to fear from death, eh?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]
- «Now, then... I've chatted enough for one lifetime. Let's get this show on the road, shall we?» [K1/Jolee_Bindo]

**[K1/Kashyyyk]**
- «Oh, I know plenty about Kashyyyk. You think someone doesn't live here for twenty years and pick up a thing or two? Chances are that by the time I finished telling you about it all, you would have found out for yourself. So there's no reason for me to start blathering on. It should be enough for me to tell you that there's kilometre after kilometre of trees on this planet. The deeper you go, the more dangerous it gets. If you're smart, you stay on top with the Wookiees. Go deep, however, and you may find some things you weren't expecting. And that's about all I got to say about that. Now let's get going.» [K1/Kashyyyk]

**[K1/Korriban]**
- «Pfah. Korriban's a rock. Mind the Sith. And that's about all I got to say about that. Now let's get going.» [K1/Korriban]

**[K1/Leviathan]**
- «I don't think I could use my Force powers to convince a whole squadron of troopers to let me go free, but maybe I can use it to get them to take me to a separate cell. They'll probably only leave a single guard to watch over an old man like me. I could use the Force to convince the guard to set me free, then I could free the rest of you.» [K1/Leviathan]
- «I've learned a few things in my time, kid. Don't underestimate the Force, for one. But it's your call. If you want to try a different plan, go ahead.» [K1/Leviathan]

**[K1/Lower_Shadowlands]**
- «Well, that is surprising. I doubt any Wookiee would support such a claim. The forest is millenia old.» [K1/Lower_Shadowlands]
- «Hmm, five years ago? I didn't notice it. This must have been installed in strictest secrecy. It couldn't have been Czerka.» [K1/Lower_Shadowlands]
- «This machine certainly seems to want a very specific type of response.» [K1/Lower_Shadowlands]
- «Well, well. A Star Map. An ancient artifact of dark side power. Can't say I'm surprised. I always knew there was something funny down here. I wonder if the Star Map has had an effect on the evolution of the creatures here in the Shadowlands. Might explain why it's so dangerous down here.» [K1/Lower_Shadowlands]
- «An interesting theory, but I suppose we don't have time to test it now, do we? Now that we've got what you came for we should be heading back up to the treetops.» [K1/Lower_Shadowlands]
- «He is almost feral after all this time. Calm yourself, Freyyr. We are friends. Don't you remember me?» [K1/Lower_Shadowlands]
- «Well, this may prove difficult.» [K1/Lower_Shadowlands]
- «Did we come this far only to kill this old wook? I don't approve of this at all.» [K1/Lower_Shadowlands]
- «I know he did, but not without reason. The only outsiders he's seen for years have been slavers.» [K1/Lower_Shadowlands]
- «Hold your tongue, child. I'm not challenging you. I just thought you'd have the smarts to consider every option.» [K1/Lower_Shadowlands]
- «Is that it? You spared his life initially just to cut him off before he can fully explain himself?» [K1/Lower_Shadowlands]
- «That's when I first saw him. I helped his pursuers lose him for a moment. Do you remember me now, Freyyr?» [K1/Lower_Shadowlands]
- «I don't approve of this! Is Chuundar to be blindly obeyed?» [K1/Lower_Shadowlands]
- «Perhaps not. Do what you will. I'm just wondering if Chuundar is the wiser choice of ally.» [K1/Lower_Shadowlands]
- «I don't support this action! Think on this, child!» [K1/Lower_Shadowlands]
- «Look, I'm not your nanny-droid, but I just don't think following Chuundar is the best course.» [K1/Lower_Shadowlands]

**[K1/Manaan]**
- «Manaan's an unusual place. Not for its water, mid you... it's the kolto. Healing juice. Only place where it occurs in the galaxy. And that's about all I got to say about that. Now let's get going.» [K1/Manaan]

**[K1/North_Beach]**
- «Wait a minute - do you understand these creatures? You must have learned their language when you came here in search of the secrets of the Star Forge. Back when you were still Revan.» [K1/North_Beach]

**[K1/Prison_Block]**
- «Guard, I need to speak with you.» [K1/Prison_Block]
- «This cell is too drafty. My old bones could catch a chill in here. We don't want that. You better let me out.» [K1/Prison_Block]
- «You shouldn't have let me out, sonny. That was wrong. Admiral Karath won't be too happy with you disobeying his orders.» [K1/Prison_Block]
- «You deserve to be locked up in the cell for disobeying orders.» [K1/Prison_Block]
- «Then I'll be sure to never let you out. Goodbye, sonny.» [K1/Prison_Block]

**[K1/Sith_Academy]**
- «You don't say? Well, the master loves tombs, don't you master?» [K1/Sith_Academy]
- «You sound very much like... someone I used to know.» [K1/Sith_Academy]
- «Hm. This should be interesting.» [K1/Sith_Academy]
- «Now where are you going with this, I wonder?» [K1/Sith_Academy]
- «It was just an idle question. Don't get so testy, damn it.» [K1/Sith_Academy]

**[K1/Sith_Academy_Entrance]**
- «Seems like not much to do to be worthy of anything. How long you been up to it, boy?» [K1/Sith_Academy_Entrance]
- «Why not just shoot them where they stand? It would at least be more direct.» [K1/Sith_Academy_Entrance]
- «Of course that's what *you* would do.» [K1/Sith_Academy_Entrance]
- «Hmm. Did you save the boy or just crush his dreams? I wonder...» [K1/Sith_Academy_Entrance]
- «One really has to wonder if all Sith start off this gullible.» [K1/Sith_Academy_Entrance]
- «Hmph. Suddenly I'm a convincing slave, am I? Maybe you should have left me on the ship.» [K1/Sith_Academy_Entrance]
- «Don't get smart-mouthed with me, young man. You're not too old to put over my knee.» [K1/Sith_Academy_Entrance]
- «Aren't we always?» [K1/Sith_Academy_Entrance]
- «If a Sith told you to go jump off a cliff, would you?» [K1/Sith_Academy_Entrance] *(+1 exact-duplicate / he-she variant line(s) merged)*
- «My hearing's not so good. I'm just pleased my nice master doesn't beat me so much anymore, yes sir.» [K1/Sith_Academy_Entrance]

**[K1/Sith_Base_(Manaan)]**
- «Well, let me guess! You intend to collect this reward, right?» [K1/Sith_Base_(Manaan)]
- «See? What did I tell you?» [K1/Sith_Base_(Manaan)]
- «*whisper* I'm not sure what kind of game you're playing, kid, but I don't understand it. Why you would help spread Sith propaganda like this is beyond me.» [K1/Sith_Base_(Manaan)]
- «Oh-ho! Is this how you win an argument? I would have thought it easier to prove the evil of the dark side than that.» [K1/Sith_Base_(Manaan)]
- «Just be careful with those gifts you've already learned. Beware the dark side... or you may end up betraying your world regardless.» [K1/Sith_Base_(Manaan)]

**[K1/Star_Forge_System]**
- «You know, during our rather rapid descent I noticed the hulls of many crashed ships scattered across the landscape. Maybe the parts you need can be found among their wreckage.» [K1/Star_Forge_System]
- «I just hope we're not too late. Bastila's been Malak's prisoner for a long time. If he turns her to the dark side she'll join him and the Sith will become invincible.» [K1/Star_Forge_System]
- «I fear Bastila will find the lure of the dark side difficult to resist. She is strong in the Force, but she is also impulsive, willful and proud - as you once were, Revan.» [K1/Star_Forge_System]
- «I remember... though I hoped you would not be so quick to embrace the being you once were. If Bastila feels as you do then she is lost to us, Revan.» [K1/Star_Forge_System]
- «I am glad to hear you say that. Now that you know your true identity I was afraid you might slip back over to the dark side. If Bastila feels as you do there may yet be hope for her.» [K1/Star_Forge_System]
- «Let us hope it does not come to that.» [K1/Star_Forge_System]
- «Hmph. Hard to say what this planet is. Been lost for thousands of years, if not more. *Sniff* Smells like bad, bad karma so watch your step. And that's about all I got to say about that. Now let's get going.» [K1/Star_Forge_System]

**[K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]**
- «Elora? Of all the people... How did you even know I'd left Kashyyyk?» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «Why? Whatever could be the matter, my dear?» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «Murder? But how...» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «Calm down, Elora. Where's Sunry now?» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «Don't worry, Elora. We'll get to the bottom of this and help Sunry... somehow.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «I suppose you're Sunry's only hope. We have to at least try to help him.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «Yes... but it almost seems too heavily stacked, doesn't it? Very suspicious.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «So nice to meet an impartial judge, for a change.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «You said it, not I.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «Speaking of that injury, has it been taken into consideration, your honor?» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «Hmmm. Simply because it was real does not mean Sunry would have overlooked it being in Elassa's hand.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «That's true... I'd forgotten about that. Let's not forget that he would have had to have been considerably older than the Sith woman.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «Hmph. I'm a Jedi... and one who was living alone in the Shadowlands until recently. That keeps you in shape, damn it! We're talking about Sunry, here.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «It's possible. I haven't seen Sunry since I crashed on Kashyyyk... a man can change a lot in that time. The man I knew could never have done this crime, however.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «Now, now, Elora. We're not here to pass judgment, my dear. We do need to know everything if we're to help, yes?» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «Elora sent me, Sunry. I'm here to get you out of this mess. The courts went and made us Arbiters for your case.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «You saved my wrinkled butt more than a few times as well, friend, if I recall. But there'll be time for catching up later. Right now we need to focus on the case.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «Soon, old friend. But we don't want to go to trial until we've got the whole picture.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «That's different, Sunry, and you know it. We don't kill them in cold blood while they sleep.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «Sunry... this is wrong, and you know it. I am a Jedi... what is it that you expect me to do to defend your actions?» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «But Sunry is a murderer. I'm sorry, old friend, but you are. I... I can't approve of this. You go ahead, however, if that's what you think is best.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «The Republic will attempt to have him freed, Elora, I am sure. This... this is the best we could do. I'm sorry.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «You defended him well enough. I wish we had had time to look into matters more fully, but wishing is only useful for the young and idealistic. What's done is done. The Republic will likely appeal the matter eventually and he'll be free. I just... I just would like to know if that's best. Sunry was a good man once. And a good friend. I'll miss him. Bah. I don't want to talk about this anymore. My jaw aches.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «I... I'm sorry, Sunry. And Elora. There is nothing more we can do.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «You did what was necessary. Sunry was guilty, as sad as that fact makes me, justice had to be done. Doesn't matter that his victim was a Sith or that he was once a hero. I only wish that my old friend hadn't done it. But that's the thing about wishes. They don't come true. And now Sunry's dead, which is a shame. He *was* a good man, you know... once. Bah. I don't want to talk about this anymore. My jaw aches.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «Well done. You showed wisdom worthy of a Jedi Master during this trial.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «We have done all we could for you, old friend.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «Don't thank me, thank your Arbiter. I was glad to help.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «Innocent, mm? I don't know what to make of it, to tell the truth. Do you think justice has been done?» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «I see. I am not so sure. If he did kill that woman, Sunry's going to have to live with that on his conscience. And if he's justified her death in his mind... well... Sunry was a good man, once. I only hope that is still the case. I enjoyed his company.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «But that has little to do with the law, does it? A sad state of affairs, that. Too bad, really... Sunry was a good man, once.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]
- «Bah. I don't want to talk about this anymore. My jaw aches.» [K1/Sunry_Murder_Trial]

**[K1/Swoop_Registration]**
- «And you're surprised that the boy doesn't want to sign?» [K1/Swoop_Registration]
- «I bet you just can't understand why they wouldn't trust someone who's only in it for the money, too.» [K1/Swoop_Registration]
- «You know, for a brute you say the sweetest things.» [K1/Swoop_Registration]
- «*ahem* Do as we say, scoundrel! You don't want to anger a shortempered Jedi!» [K1/Swoop_Registration] *(+1 exact-duplicate / he-she variant line(s) merged)*
- «Tsk tsk. Not even willing to expend a bit of effort in actual persuasion, hey? Well, you do what you like.» [K1/Swoop_Registration]

**[K1/Tatooine]**
- «Well, unless you're one of the local species and end up being slaughtered or enslaved, Tatooine's a nice enough desert. And that's about all I got to say about that. Now let's get going.» [K1/Tatooine]

**[K1/Temple_Exterior]**
- «Wait... you can't go in there alone!» [K1/Temple_Exterior]
- «I had a... a premonition. A vision through the Force. There's great danger inside that Temple. I can't let you face it alone.» [K1/Temple_Exterior]
- «You might be walking into a trap... maybe Malak himself is waiting inside. Even if he isn't, that temple will be crawling with Dark Jedi. You'll need all the help you can get.» [K1/Temple_Exterior]
- «Your destiny - maybe the fate of the entire galaxy - could be forever changed inside that Temple. I'm not about to let you face that alone. Not after my premonition. There's a reason I had that vision.» [K1/Temple_Exterior]
- «So you just tell that guide of yours to do whatever he has to do to get us all inside the Temple.» [K1/Temple_Exterior]
- «I don't know what he said, but it sounds like your guide's being stubborn. We don't have time for this. The Republic fleet is on the way and we're stuck on this planet until we deactivate that disruptor field. You have to convince him to get us inside that Temple!» [K1/Temple_Exterior]
- «Not exactly the way I would have handled it, but given the circumstances it'll do.» [K1/Temple_Exterior]
- «Why do I get the feeling this is going to take a while? Still, we better stay ready. As soon as those shields go down we have to get inside the temple.» [K1/Temple_Exterior]

**[K1/Temple_Summit]**
- «Hold on - you have to disable the energy shield around this Temple before we can leave. I saw a computer up here. It might be connected to the power generators of this place. And if you don't disable the disruptor field protecting the Star Forge, the Ebon Hawk won't be able to leave the planet without crashing.» [K1/Temple_Summit]
- «Come on, Bastila. We have to escape before Malak shows up!» [K1/Temple_Summit]
- «It's not to late for you, Bastila. The dark side is evil but it isn't all powerful. You can still fight against it. Don't let it consume you.» [K1/Temple_Summit]
- «Listen to me... the dark side leads to death and destruction. I've seen the horrors the Sith have unleashed on the galaxy. Turn away from this path.» [K1/Temple_Summit]
- «Don't do this, kid. I don't want to, but I'll fight you if I have to. Even if it costs me my life.» [K1/Temple_Summit]
- «I'm a Jedi... I'll never bow down to the Sith. If you go through with this I have no choice but to do battle against you.» [K1/Temple_Summit]
- «No! Don't give into your anger. Remain calm, remain focused. Don't let her twist your mind like this!» [K1/Temple_Summit]
- «She's not dead, Carth - though that might have been easier for you to accept. She's fallen to the dark side and fled to the Star Forge.» [K1/Temple_Summit]
- «She was always in danger of being seduced by the dark side, Carth. Bastila was strong, but she was always impatient and headstrong. Malak preyed upon her weaknesses. This planet is a tainted place, the Star Forge and the Temple have twisted the Force into an instrument of evil - just as Malak has twisted Bastila into a servant of the Sith.» [K1/Temple_Summit]
- «Malak has a strong hold on Bastila now - it will be difficult for her to break free of his influence. Especially considering her long association with you.» [K1/Temple_Summit]
- «Weakness has nothing to do with the corruption of the dark side. A Jedi must always guard against pride and the desire for power. Especially you.» [K1/Temple_Summit]
- «In the end we all choose our own path, but keep in mind the role that you played in her downfall. You and the task assigned to her by the Jedi Council.» [K1/Temple_Summit]
- «Remember the bond that was forged between you when she rekindled the spark that was your life. Through that bond she touched your memories... and also the echo of the dark taint within you.» [K1/Temple_Summit]
- «I'm not sure what's in store for us, but I sense Bastila still has a role to play. I have no doubt she'll be waiting for us on the Star Forge.» [K1/Temple_Summit]
- «No doubt. I must say that it's good to have you with us, my dear. For a minute there I thought you might decide... well, never mind what I thought.» [K1/Temple_Summit]
- «Bastila tried to tempt her to the dark side, to reclaim Revan's heritage. She failed.» [K1/Temple_Summit]
- «Well, I'm an old man and I know you should take love when you can find it. Good for you two, I say.» [K1/Temple_Summit]

**[K1/Tomb_of_Ajunta_Pall]**
- «Now this you don't see every day. This... this is an old spirit full of the Force. I sense great sadness and regret. Walk carefully...» [K1/Tomb_of_Ajunta_Pall]
- «Hmph. The Sith consumed themselves, did they? Hm. I suppose that eventually that'd be all they'd have left to fight.» [K1/Tomb_of_Ajunta_Pall]
- «Hmph. Typical. Always ready to jump at what they perceive to be the easier path.» [K1/Tomb_of_Ajunta_Pall]

**[K1/Tomb_of_Marka_Ragnos]**
- «Well... so much for the sanctity of life?» [K1/Tomb_of_Marka_Ragnos]
- «Heh. It shows innovative something, that's for sure.» [K1/Tomb_of_Marka_Ragnos]

**[K1/Tomb_of_Tulak_Hord]**
- «There's always remorse. And atonement. That's the harder path, though, boy. Think you can do that?» [K1/Tomb_of_Tulak_Hord]

**[K1/Upper_Shadowlands]**
- «Ah, the damnable racket of battle! Watch yourself, even more of these crawling beasts are hiding in the underbrush.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «I'm Jolee. Jolee Bindo. Follow me to my camp and we'll talk a bit.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Why am I here? Hmph. Well it isn't because I like the stink of dead kinrath. Let's move this talk to my camp where the air is better.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Ah, don't start fawning just yet, I'm too old for it. *Sigh* I...I know a few things, but we can talk about it at my camp.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Look, you can follow me now or stumble around on your own, but I'm too old to care whether you think I'm babysitting you.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Keep close. It's nearby under a log. Yeah, I live like some burrowing rodent. I fought the Sith... now look at me. Hmph!» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «I'm sure you are, but you have to take time to listen. Really, you shouldn't try so hard to make new enemies.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Do you? Well, I'd better not keep you. I mean, I've only spent about twenty years here. Heh. What could I possibly tell you?» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Hmph! You don't want to be just another fool with a big impatient chip on their shoulder. That's a dark road, child.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Yes, yes. I suppose it's my lot in life, to spend years learning something so that someone else can be impatient to hear it.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Oh ho, your manner is fearsome. Like the little ullers I shoo from the garden, hissing at what they don't understand.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «I'm too old for your posturing, child. If you have questions, then ask them.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Don't coddle me, child. I'm neither a Jedi nor your master. I'm just an old man that's been lost in the woods for far too long.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «You'll have nothing but time if you don't take what help you can. I've been here for many years. I know things. Remember that.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «My days of glory are behind me, but... perhaps there is something I can do for you. You must have questions.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Now why would you be asking about that, hmm? Don't answer. I knew that had to be why you were here.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Ahh, I thought you would spot that. It's easy to see with Force-sensitive eyes, isn't it? Heh heh. Czerka put it there. It's a troublesome thing, blocking some access to the lower Shadowlands. That's where you want to go, isn't it?» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Wait wait wait, slow down. I have to ask something of you. I've got something you need, and you can help me. We both know why you have come here. The Star Map. That has to be it. There is nothing else here.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «The problems of a few Wookiees don't amount to anything before the concerns of the Jedi. No, you are here for the map.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Kashyyyk is an interesting place, more so than anyone suspects. If Czerka Corporation knew, heh, the planet would be a strip mine. The Wookiees have their legends that they were not always here, but it is more than that. The trees themselves are strangers» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «What I'm saying is that there are literally walls in your way, and you won't find what you need without my help... and my help has a price.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «[[../Upper Shadowlands#Star Map: Kashyyyk» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Now, I'm not sure if you'll measure up to my task, seeing as how you've already joined up with the slime I want gone.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «The Czerka poachers. I can smell that scent they use on you. I don't care what you told them, I want them gone.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Hunting monkey is part of your great plan? I know you talked to the Czerka poachers, and I don't care. I want them gone. After you do that, I want to join up with you...» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «After you do that, I want to join up with you. Then I'll remove the barrier between you and the lower Shadowlands.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «You must do a task for me, and then allow me to join with you. I will then remove certain barriers in your path.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Oh, save it. I know this sounds absurd, but I'm old and entitled to work you around a bit. Besides, the test is simple.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Since they began expanding in the Shadowlands, the Czerka have left me alone, for the most part. Until recently, anyway. A group of them set up camp not far from here. Poachers is all they are. I'd like them removed from this place.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «They don't respect this place. They are butchering wildlife and threatening entire species. It's getting under my skin. The Wookiees could take care of themselves if they wished, but the animals are truly helpless victims.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Oh please, this is arbitrary errand work, but such are the requests of the aged.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Yes, yes, I'm an old man that's getting fussy. Call me foolish if you will, I don't care.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «They are not that far northeast of here. There is usually five in the area, as far as I can see. The Captain of the lot is the one that earned my ire. Mishandle my garden, will he? Hmph!» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «You presumably have a ship. I've seen all I wish to here. Isn't that enough? I mean, I'm really sick of the trees. And perhaps your destiny might show me something new. You never know.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Don't talk back to your elders, child. Look, they know me quite well, and are always on alert when I approach. The only way I could get rid of them would be violence, which would eventually lead to trouble for the Wookiees. I want them gone, but with as little fuss as possible. They have already caused enough trouble as it is.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «You still have to do it. Their camp is in the far northeast. Now remember, if I wanted them dead, I'd have done it myself. Find a better way.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Good. Return to me here and we will see how you have done. Shoo. Shoo!» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Welcome back. I wonder, have you had much luck? Have you done as I asked?» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «*Sigh* Fine, fine, just be brief.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «They are not hard to find. Their camp is to the far northeast. Try to get them to leave without too much fuss. After you finish, I will make it so you can pass the barrier that prevents your access to the lower Shadowlands.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «I smell their tach-scent on you. Did you agree to hunt for them? Let me remind you that I do not approve, and you need me. Go to their camp and get rid of them. When you are through, we can proceed to the lower Shadowlands. Not before.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «I follow the Jedi Way and I command the Force, yes, and I suppose that makes me as much a Jedi as anyone... but what of it? Why should being what I am compel me to entertain others with tales of the 'good old days' huh?» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Did I? You have annoyingly good ears. It doesn't matter. Those days are gone. Leave them in their graves.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Why, don't you like what I've done with the place? The creatures here aren't any more treacherous than elsewhere.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Perhaps you will be of some assistance to me after all. Let us discuss what you came here for.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Ah, and it makes you think I may have the taint of the dark side in me, doesn't it? Ooh. Is that what you are wondering, huh? Ooh.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Well, I assure you, I see more grey than dark or light. I'm just a stubborn old man, tired of the foolishness of others.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Look, we've been through this before. My past is my affair. Get some years behind you and then we'll talk about me. I think you'll agree that we have more important things to discuss, no?» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «I told you my price. After you complete my task I'll make it so you can pass the barriers blocking your goal. That's all I will add on the subject. I saw them put it in, so only I know how to manipulate it. That knowledge will serve me now. Do as I ask.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Well hurry up! You don't want to be stuck here as long as I have.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Yes, among the Wookiees. Intriguing creatures. I like that they have little patience for bureaucrats. But of course, even here there are hidden things that manipulate.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
- «Czerka Corporation was smart to put him in power. He's as good at destroying Wookiee culture as dropping corpses full of Ardroxian Flu. Will you work for him or against him, I wonder? I will be interested to see.» [K1/Upper_Shadowlands]
